// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use core::time::Duration;

use ostd::sync::{WaitQueue, Waiter};

use super::{clocks::JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER, timer::Timeout, Timer, TimerManager};
use crate::prelude::*;

/// A trait that provide the timeout related function for [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`]`.
pub trait WaitTimeout {
    /// Waits until some condition returns `Some(_)` or a given timeout is reached.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the condition does not become `Some(_)` before the timeout is reached, this function
    /// will return an error with [`ETIME`].
    ///
    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    #[track_caller]
    fn wait_until_or_timeout<'a, F, T, R>(&self, mut cond: F, timeout: T) -> Result<R>
    where
        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
        T: Into<TimeoutExt<'a>>,
    {
        let timeout = timeout.into();
        let timeout_inner = match timeout.check_expired() {
            Ok(inner) => inner,
            Err(err) => return cond().ok_or(err),
        };

        self.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, || Ok(()), timeout_inner)
    }

    /// Waits until some condition returns `Some(_)` or is cancelled by the timeout or cancel
    /// condition.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the condition does not become `Some(_)` before the cancellation, this function
    /// will return:
    ///  - an error with [`ETIME`] if the timeout is reached;
    ///  - the error returned by the cancel condition if the cancel condition returns `Err(_)`.
    #[doc(hidden)]
    #[track_caller]
    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
        &self,
        cond: F,
        cancel_cond: FCancel,
        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    ) -> Result<R>
    where
        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>;
}

/// A timeout with extended semantics.
pub enum TimeoutExt<'a> {
    /// The timeout will never fire.
    Never,
    /// The timeout will expire later according to [`ManagedTimeout`].
    At(ManagedTimeout<'a>),
}

impl<'a> TimeoutExt<'a> {
    /// Checks whether the timeout is expired.
    ///
    /// This method will return:
    ///  - `Ok(Some(_))` if the timeout isn't expired but it may be expired later.
    ///  - `Ok(None)` if the timeout will never be expired.
    ///  - `Err(ETIME)` if the timeout is expired.
    pub fn check_expired(&self) -> Result<Option<&ManagedTimeout<'a>>> {
        match self {
            TimeoutExt::At(inner) if inner.is_expired() => {
                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the time limit is reached")
            }
            TimeoutExt::At(inner) => Ok(Some(inner)),
            TimeoutExt::Never => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// Freezes the expired time.
    ///
    /// This works in the same way as [`ManagedTimeout::freeze`].
    pub fn freeze(&mut self) {
        match self {
            Self::Never => (),
            Self::At(timeout) => timeout.freeze(),
        }
    }
}

impl From<&Duration> for TimeoutExt<'_> {
    fn from(value: &Duration) -> Self {
        Self::At(ManagedTimeout::new(*value))
    }
}

impl From<Option<&Duration>> for TimeoutExt<'_> {
    fn from(value: Option<&Duration>) -> Self {
        match value {
            Some(duration) => duration.into(),
            None => Self::Never,
        }
    }
}

impl<'a> From<ManagedTimeout<'a>> for TimeoutExt<'a> {
    fn from(value: ManagedTimeout<'a>) -> Self {
        Self::At(value)
    }
}

impl<'a> From<Option<ManagedTimeout<'a>>> for TimeoutExt<'a> {
    fn from(value: Option<ManagedTimeout<'a>>) -> Self {
        match value {
            Some(timeout) => timeout.into(),
            None => Self::Never,
        }
    }
}

/// A [`Timeout`] with the associated [`TimerManager`].
pub struct ManagedTimeout<'a> {
    timeout: Timeout,
    manager: &'a Arc<TimerManager>,
}

impl<'a> ManagedTimeout<'a> {
    /// Creates a new `ManagedTimeout` with the JIFFIES timer manager.
    pub fn new(timeout: Duration) -> Self {
        let timeout = Timeout::After(timeout);
        let manager = JIFFIES_TIMER_MANAGER.get().unwrap();
        Self::new_with_manager(timeout, manager)
    }

    /// Creates a new `ManagedTimeout` with the given timer manager.
    pub const fn new_with_manager(timeout: Timeout, manager: &'a Arc<TimerManager>) -> Self {
        Self { timeout, manager }
    }

    /// Returns weather the timeout is expired.
    pub fn is_expired(&self) -> bool {
        self.manager.is_expired_timeout(&self.timeout)
    }

    /// Freezes the expired time.
    ///
    /// If the timeout is specified as an instant after a period of time from the current time
    /// (i.e., [`Timeout::After`]), this method will freeze the timeout by converting it to a fixed
    /// instant (i.e., [`Timeout::When`]).
    pub fn freeze(&mut self) {
        self.timeout = match self.timeout {
            Timeout::When(instant) => Timeout::When(instant),
            Timeout::After(duration) => Timeout::When(self.manager.clock().read_time() + duration),
        }
    }

    /// Creates a timer for the timeout.
    pub fn create_timer<F>(&self, callback: F) -> Arc<Timer>
    where
        F: Fn() + Send + Sync + 'static,
    {
        let timer = self.manager.create_timer(callback);
        timer.set_timeout(self.timeout.clone());
        timer
    }
}

impl WaitTimeout for Waiter {
    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
        &self,
        cond: F,
        cancel_cond: FCancel,
        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    ) -> Result<R>
    where
        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>,
    {
        // No fast paths for `Waiter`. If the caller wants a fast path, it should do so _before_
        // the waiter is created.

        let timer = timeout.map(|timeout| {
            let waker = self.waker();
            timeout.create_timer(move || {
                waker.wake_up();
            })
        });

        let cancel_cond = {
            let timer = timer.clone();

            move || {
                if timer
                    .as_ref()
                    .is_some_and(|timer| timer.remain() == Duration::ZERO)
                {
                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the time limit is reached");
                }

                cancel_cond()
            }
        };

        let res = self.wait_until_or_cancelled(cond, cancel_cond);

        // If `res` is not `ETIME` error, then the timeout may not have been expired.
        // We cancel it manually.
        if let Some(timer) = timer
            && !res
                .as_ref()
                .is_err_and(|e: &Error| e.error() == Errno::ETIME)
        {
            timer.cancel();
        }

        res
    }
}

impl WaitTimeout for WaitQueue {
    fn wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled<F, R, FCancel>(
        &self,
        mut cond: F,
        cancel_cond: FCancel,
        timeout: Option<&ManagedTimeout>,
    ) -> Result<R>
    where
        F: FnMut() -> Option<R>,
        FCancel: Fn() -> Result<()>,
    {
        // Fast path:
        if let Some(res) = cond() {
            return Ok(res);
        }

        let (waiter, _) = Waiter::new_pair();
        let cond = || {
            self.enqueue(waiter.waker());
            cond()
        };
        waiter.wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled(cond, cancel_cond, timeout)
    }
}

impl Poller {
    /// Constructs a new poller to wait for interesting events.
    ///
    /// If `timeout` is specified, [`Self::wait`] will fail with [`ETIME`] after the specified
    /// timeout is expired.
    ///
    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    pub fn new(timeout: Option<&Duration>) -> Self {
        let (waiter, waker) = Waiter::new_pair();

        let mut timeout_ext = TimeoutExt::from(timeout);
        timeout_ext.freeze();

        Self {
            poller: PollHandle::new(Arc::downgrade(&waker) as Weak<_>),
            waiter,
            timeout: timeout_ext,
        }
    }

    /// Returns a mutable reference of [`PollHandle`].
    pub fn as_handle_mut(&mut self) -> &mut PollHandle {
        &mut self.poller
    }

    /// Waits until some interesting events happen since the last wait.
    ///
    /// This method will fail with [`EINTR`] if interrupted by signals or [`ETIME`] on timeout.
    ///
    /// [`EINTR`]: crate::error::Errno::EINTR
    /// [`ETIME`]: crate::error::Errno::ETIME
    pub fn wait(&self) -> Result<()> {
        self.waiter.pause_timeout(&self.timeout)
    }
}

impl Observer<IoEvents> for Waker {
    fn on_events(&self, _events: &IoEvents) {
        self.wake_up();
    }
}

/// The `Pollable` trait allows for waiting for events and performing event-based operations.
///
/// Implementors are required to provide a method, [`Pollable::poll`], which is usually implemented
/// by simply calling [`Pollable::poll`] on the internal [`Pollee`]. This trait provides another
/// method, [`Pollable::wait_events`], to allow waiting for events and performing operations
/// according to the events.
///

## Maintainability

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 1

> ```diff

> ```

`accurate-names` (nit): The workflow `name` literal is `Test x86-64  ` with two trailing spaces inside the string, so the Actions UI label differs from the visible name readers expect.

**Fix.** Remove the trailing spaces, e.g. `name: Test x86-64`.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 18

> ```diff
> 17	      matrix:

`information-hiding` (minor): The `id` matrix axis is an implicit input to `.github/actions/test/action.yml`: the composite action reads `${{ matrix.id }}` directly, while this caller only passes `auto_test`. A future caller can rename `id` or reuse the action without discovering that hidden dependency from the `with` block.

**Fix.** Expose the selected basic test as an explicit action input, for example `basic_test: ${{ matrix.basic_test }}`, and have `.github/actions/test/action.yml` read `inputs.basic_test` instead of `matrix.id`.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 41

`accurate-names` (minor): `test_id` says `boot-multiboot2-smp4`, but the adjacent comment and `boot_protocol` both say this case runs `multiboot`. That label will mislead anyone triaging boot protocol coverage from the matrix name.

**Fix.** Make the label and configuration agree: rename the case to `boot-multiboot-smp4` if `boot_protocol: 'multiboot'` is intended, or change `boot_protocol` and the comment to `multiboot2` if that is the intended test.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 102

> ```diff

`no-bool-args` (major): `is_seqpacket` is a boolean behavior selector for `try_read` and `try_write`, and it drives separate stream-vs-seqpacket paths throughout the shared implementation. Call sites pass `self.is_seqpacket`, so the requested semantics are not clear without reading the callee.

**Fix.** Replace the flag with a typed mode such as `UnixStreamMode::{Stream, Seqpacket}`, or split the behavior into separate `try_read_stream`/`try_read_seqpacket` and `try_write_stream`/`try_write_seqpacket` helpers.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 238

**Fix.** Remove the dead `is_seqpacket` check from this `!is_seqpacket` fast path, or factor the common write helper so the seqpacket capacity check appears only where seqpacket writes are possible.

`least-surprise` (major): `Pause` exposes `pause_timeout` for every implementor, but `WaitQueue::pause_timeout` below panics with `pause_timeout can only be used on Waiter`. A trait method that is intentionally unsupported forces callers to remember implementor-specific exceptions instead of trusting the trait contract.

**Fix.** Move `pause_timeout` to an inherent `Waiter` method or a waiter-only extension trait, or provide a real `WaitQueue` implementation instead of exposing an unsupported trait method.

## Correctness

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 84

> ```diff
>   release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
>   enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ...
>   release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
>   enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ```

Incorrect fallback (major): `matrix.release || true` turns the explicit `release: false` rows into `true`, because `false` is the left operand of `||`. The `syscall-debug` and `syscall-ext2-iommu-debug` jobs therefore run release builds instead of debug builds, and the same broken expression is repeated for the `gVisor` step at line `99`.

**Fix.** Preserve explicit `false` values. For example, give every included matrix row an explicit `release` value and pass `release: ${{ matrix.release }}` in both integration-test steps.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 85

> ```diff
>   release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
>   enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ...
>   release: ${{ matrix.release || true }}
>   enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}
> ```

Incorrect fallback (major): `matrix.enable_kvm || true` also turns the explicit `enable_kvm: false` row into `true`, so `syscall-exfat-multiboot2-nokvm` still runs with KVM enabled. The `gVisor` step repeats the same expression at line `100`.

**Fix.** Preserve explicit `false` values. For example, set `enable_kvm` on all matrix entries and pass `enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm }}` in both integration-test steps.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 113

> ```diff
> matrix:
>   image: ['asterinas/asterinas:0.16.0-20250802', 'asterinas/osdk:0.16.0-20250802']

> container:

Wrong matrix key (major): The OSDK matrix defines only `matrix.image`, but `.github/actions/test/action.yml` branches on `matrix.container` in the `Run OSDK tests` step. That makes the `asterinas/osdk` row take the non-OSDK branch and run `make check_osdk` when it should be skipped for that image.

**Fix.** Use one name consistently, or pass the image through an action input. For example, rename the matrix key to `container` and use `image: ${{ matrix.container }}`, or add an `image` input to `.github/actions/test/action.yml` and branch on `inputs.image`.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 202

> ```diff
> let ctrl_msgs = aux_data.generate_control(is_pass_cred);
> debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0);
> peer_end
>     .has_aux
>     .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

Reachable panic (major): A valid zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` receive reaches this `debug_assert!`: `send(..., len = 0)` queues an auxiliary range with `start == end`, `try_read()` pops it with `read_tot_len == 0`, and a non-empty receive buffer makes `is_empty` false. Debug builds then panic instead of returning `Ok((0, ...))`.

**Fix.** Allow zero-length sequenced packets in the invariant, or remove this assertion. For example, include `is_seqpacket` in the condition: `debug_assert!(is_empty || is_seqpacket || read_tot_len != 0);`.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 315

> ```diff
> if !self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
>     events |= IoEvents::IN;
> }
> ```

Lost readiness (major): `check_io_events()` reports `IN` only when the byte ring is non-empty, but a zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message is stored only in `peer_end.all_aux` with no bytes in `reader`. A task already blocked in `recvmsg()` or `poll()` can sleep forever after such a packet is sent, because the send path bypasses `write_with()` for `write_len == 0` and no `IN` readiness is observable.

**Fix.** Represent zero-length sequenced packets in the readiness state and wake the peer when one is queued. For example, have `check_io_events()` treat a queued zero-length packet as `IoEvents::IN`, and notify the peer when `try_write()` pushes that packet.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154

> ```diff
> if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
>     posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>     self.wait();
>     posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
> } else {
>     self.wait();
> }
> ```

Missed pending signal (major): `pause_timeout()` installs the signal waker and immediately calls `self.wait()` without checking whether `posix_thread.has_pending()` is already true. If a signal became pending before `set_signalled_waker()`, no later signal enqueue occurs to wake this waiter; with `TimeoutExt::Never` from `Poller::new(None)`, `poll()`/`epoll_wait()` can block indefinitely instead of returning `EINTR`.

**Fix.** Check the cancel condition before sleeping, or reuse `wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled()` with a cancel closure that returns `EINTR` when `posix_thread.has_pending()` is true.

## Security

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 219

> ```diff
>     let old_mask = sig_mask.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
>     sig_mask.store(mask_op(old_mask), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 
>     // Perform the operation.
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `with_sigmask_changed` stores the temporary mask returned by `mask_op` verbatim. Callers such as `sys_ppoll` and `sys_pselect6` pass user-controlled masks, so a process can call `ppoll`/`pselect6` with `SIGKILL` or `SIGSTOP` set and then block indefinitely; local delivery uses `has_signal_blocked`/`has_pending`, so those mandatory signals remain pending instead of killing or stopping the thread. Linux documents that `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` cannot be blocked ([`sigprocmask(2)`](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/sigprocmask.2.html), [`signal(7)`](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/signal.7.html)).

**Fix.** Strip `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` from the computed mask before storing it, matching `sys_rt_sigprocmask` and `sys_rt_sigsuspend`.

```rust
use super::constants::{SIGKILL, SIGSTOP};

let mut new_mask = mask_op(old_mask);
new_mask -= SIGKILL;
new_mask -= SIGSTOP;
sig_mask.store(new_mask, Ordering::Relaxed);
```

>     start: write_start,
>     end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
> };
> all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
> ```

/// The original methods of `Waiter` and `WaitQueue` only allow a thread to wait

    98	
    99	    pub(super) fn try_read(
   100	        &self,
   101	        writer: &mut dyn MultiWrite,
   102	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   103	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
   104	        let is_empty = writer.is_empty();
   105	        if is_empty && !is_seqpacket {
   106	            if self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   107	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EAGAIN, "the channel is empty");
   108	            }
   109	            return Ok((0, Vec::new()));
   110	        }
   111	
   112	        let mut reader = self.reader.lock();
   113	        // `reader.len()` is an `Acquire` operation. So it can guarantee that the `has_aux`
   114	        // check below sees the up-to-date value.
   115	        let no_aux_len = reader.len();
   116	
   117	        let peer_end = self.inner.peer_end();
   118	        let is_pass_cred = self.inner.this_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   119	
   120	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to receive.
   121	        if !peer_end.has_aux.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   122	            let read_len = self
   123	                .inner
   124	                .read_with(move || reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, no_aux_len))?;
   125	            let ctrl_msgs = if is_pass_cred {
   126	                AuxiliaryData::default().generate_control(is_pass_cred)
   127	            } else {
   128	                Vec::new()
   129	            };
   130	            return Ok((read_len, ctrl_msgs));
   131	        }
   132	
   133	        let mut all_aux = peer_end.all_aux.lock();
   134	        let mut aux_prev_data: Option<AuxiliaryData> = None;
   135	        let mut read_tot_len = 0;
   136	
   137	        let aux_data = loop {
   138	            let read_start = reader.head();
   139	            let (aux_len, aux_front) = if let Some(front) = all_aux.front_mut() {
   140	                if front.start == read_start {
   141	                    ((front.end - read_start).0, Some(front))
   142	                } else {
   143	                    ((front.start - read_start).0, None)
   144	                }
   145	            } else {
   146	                (usize::MAX, None)
   147	            };
   148	
   149	            // Unless the auxiliary data we have already received is a subset of the current
   150	            // auxiliary data, we cannot receive additional bytes.
   151	            if let Some(prev) = aux_prev_data.as_mut() {
   152	                let is_subset = if let Some(front) = aux_front.as_ref() {
   153	                    prev.is_subset_of(&front.data, is_pass_cred)
   154	                } else {
   155	                    prev.is_subset_of(&AuxiliaryData::default(), is_pass_cred)
   156	                };
   157	                if !is_subset {
   158	                    break prev;
   159	                }
   160	            }
   161	
   162	            // Read the payload bytes of the current auxiliary data.
   163	            let read_res = if !is_empty && aux_len > 0 {
   164	                self.inner
   165	                    .read_with(|| reader.read_fallible_with_max_len(writer, aux_len))
   166	            } else {
   167	                Ok(0)
   168	            };
   169	            let read_len = match read_res {
   170	                Ok(read_len) => read_len,
   171	                Err(_) if read_tot_len > 0 => break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap(),
   172	                Err(err) => return Err(err),
   173	            };
   174	            read_tot_len += read_len;
   175	
   176	            // Record the current auxiliary data. Break if the read is incomplete or this is a
   177	            // `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socket.
   178	            if is_seqpacket {
   179	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   180	                if read_len < aux_len {
   181	                    warn!("setting MSG_TRUNC is not supported");
   182	                    reader.skip(aux_len - read_len);
   183	                }
   184	                break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   185	            } else if let Some(front) = aux_front {
   186	                if read_len < aux_len {
   187	                    front.start += read_len;
   188	                    break &mut front.data;
   189	                }
   190	                aux_prev_data = Some(all_aux.pop_front().unwrap().data);
   191	            } else {
   192	                aux_prev_data = Some(AuxiliaryData::default());
   193	                if read_len < aux_len {
   194	                    break aux_prev_data.as_mut().unwrap();
   195	                }
   196	            }
   197	        };
   198	
   199	        drop(reader);
   200	
   201	        let ctrl_msgs = aux_data.generate_control(is_pass_cred);
   202	        debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0);
   203	        peer_end
   204	            .has_aux
   205	            .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   206	
   207	        Ok((read_tot_len, ctrl_msgs))
   208	    }
   209	
   210	    pub(super) fn try_write(
   211	        &self,
   212	        reader: &mut dyn MultiRead,
   213	        aux_data: &mut AuxiliaryData,
   214	        is_seqpacket: bool,
   215	    ) -> Result<usize> {
   216	        let is_empty = reader.is_empty();
   217	        if is_empty {
   218	            if self.inner.is_shutdown() {
   219	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPIPE, "the channel is shut down");
   220	            }
   221	            if !is_seqpacket {
   222	                return Ok(0);
   223	            }
   224	        }
   225	
   226	        if is_seqpacket && reader.sum_lens() >= UNIX_STREAM_DEFAULT_BUF_SIZE {
   227	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EMSGSIZE, "the message is too large");
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        let this_end = self.inner.this_end();
   231	        let need_pass_cred = this_end.is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   232	            || self.inner.peer_end().is_pass_cred.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   233	
   234	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to transmit.
   235	        if aux_data.is_empty() && !is_seqpacket && !need_pass_cred {
   236	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   237	            return self.inner.write_with(move || {
   238	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   239	                    return Ok(0);
   240	                }
   241	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   242	            });
   243	        }
   244	
   245	        let mut all_aux = this_end.all_aux.lock();
   246	
   247	        // No matter we succeed later or not, set the flag first to ensure that the auxiliary
   248	        // data are always visible to `try_recv`.
   249	        this_end.has_aux.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   250	
   251	        // Write the payload bytes.
   252	        let (write_start, write_res) = if !is_empty {
   253	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
   254	            let write_start = writer.tail();
   255	            let write_res = self.inner.write_with(move || {
   256	                if is_seqpacket && writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
   257	                    return Ok(0);
   258	                }
   259	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
   260	            });
   261	            (write_start, write_res)
   262	        } else {
   263	            (self.writer.lock().tail(), Ok(0))
   264	        };
   265	        let Ok(write_len) = write_res else {
   266	            this_end
   267	                .has_aux
   268	                .store(!all_aux.is_empty(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   269	            return write_res;
   270	        };
   271	
   272	        if need_pass_cred {
   273	            aux_data.fill_cred();
   274	        }
   275	
   276	        // Store the auxiliary data.
   277	        let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
   278	            data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
   279	            start: write_start,
   280	            end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
   281	        };
   282	        all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
   283	
   284	        Ok(write_len)
   285	    }
   286	
   287	    pub(super) fn shutdown(&self, cmd: SockShutdownCmd) {
   288	        if cmd.shut_read() {
   289	            self.inner.peer_shutdown();
   290	        }
   291	
   292	        if cmd.shut_write() {
   293	            self.inner.shutdown();
   294	        }
   295	    }
   296	
   297	    pub(super) fn is_read_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   298	        self.inner.is_peer_shutdown()
   299	    }
   300	
   301	    pub(super) fn is_write_shutdown(&self) -> bool {
   302	        self.inner.is_shutdown()
   303	    }
   304	
   305	    pub(super) fn set_pass_cred(&self, is_pass_cred: bool) {
   306	        self.inner
   307	            .this_end()
   308	            .is_pass_cred
   309	            .store(is_pass_cred, Ordering::Relaxed);
   310	    }
   311	
   312	    pub(super) fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
   313	        let mut events = IoEvents::empty();
   314	
   315	        if !self.reader.lock().is_empty() {
   316	            events |= IoEvents::IN;
   317	        }
   318	
   319	        if !self.writer.lock().is_full() {
   320	            events |= IoEvents::OUT;
   321	        }
   322	
   323	        events
   324	    }
   325	
   326	    pub(super) fn cloned_pollee(&self) -> Pollee {
   327	        self.inner.this_end().state.cloned_pollee()
   328	    }

   330	    pub(super) fn peer_cred(&self) -> &SocketCred {

   330	///
   331	/// This trait is added instead of creating a new method in [`Pollee`] because sometimes we do not
   332	/// have access to the internal [`Pollee`], but there is a method that provides the same semantics
   333	/// as [`Pollable::poll`] and we need to perform event-based operations using that method.
   334	pub trait Pollable {
   335	    /// Returns the interesting events now and monitors their occurrence in the future if the
   336	    /// poller is provided.
   337	    ///
   338	    /// This method has the same semantics as [`Pollee::poll_with`].
   339	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents;
   340	
   341	    /// Waits for events and performs event-based operations.
   342	    ///
   343	    /// If a call to `try_op()` succeeds or fails with an error code other than `EAGAIN`, the
   344	    /// method will return whatever the call to `try_op()` returns. Otherwise, the method will wait
   345	    /// for some interesting events specified in `mask` to happen and try again.
   346	    ///
   347	    /// This method will fail with `ETIME` if the timeout is specified and the event does not occur
   348	    /// before the timeout expires.
   349	    ///
   350	    /// The user must ensure that a call to `try_op()` does not fail with `EAGAIN` when the
   351	    /// interesting events occur. However, it is allowed to have spurious `EAGAIN` failures due to
   352	    /// race opitions where the events are consumed by another thread.
   353	    #[track_caller]
   354	    fn wait_events<F, R>(
   355	        &self,
   356	        mask: IoEvents,
   357	        timeout: Option<&Duration>,
   358	        mut try_op: F,
   359	    ) -> Result<R>
   360	    where
   361	        Self: Sized,
   362	        F: FnMut() -> Result<R>,
   363	    {
   364	        // Fast path: Return immediately if the operation gives a result.
   365	        match try_op() {
   366	            Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::EAGAIN => (),
   367	            result => return result,
   368	        }
   369	
   370	        // Fast path: Return immediately if the timeout is zero.
   371	        if timeout.is_some_and(|duration| duration.is_zero()) {
   372	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ETIME, "the timeout expired");
   373	        }
   374	
   375	        // Create the poller and register to wait for the events.
   376	        let mut poller = Poller::new(timeout);
   377	        if self.poll(mask, Some(poller.as_handle_mut())).is_empty() {
   378	            poller.wait()?;
   379	        }
   380	
   381	        loop {
   382	            // Try again after the event happens.
   383	            match try_op() {
   384	                Err(err) if err.error() == Errno::EAGAIN => (),
   385	                result => return result,
   386	            };
   387	
   388	            // Wait until the next event happens.
   389	            poller.wait()?;
   390	        }
   391	    }
   392	}
   393

   405

   407	            pollee.poll_with(IoEvents::all(), None, || IoEvents::IN),
   408	            // This is allowed, as we invoke the checking closure.
   409	            IoEvents::IN
   410	        );
   411

   413	            pollee.poll_with(IoEvents::all(), None, || IoEvents::OUT),
   414	            // This is allowed, as the cached state is still valid.
   415	            IoEvents::IN
   416	        );
   417	    }

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The reviewed files keep most of the new behavior localized, but several issues are worth fixing before relying on this tree. The highest-impact problems are in the workflow defaults and the wait/socket edge cases: explicit `false` matrix values are converted back to `true`, the OSDK action branch reads the wrong matrix key, zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` messages are not represented in readiness, and `pause_timeout()` can miss an already-pending signal. The security-sensitive issue is that temporary syscall masks can include `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`, which Linux requires to remain unblockable.
+
+Structurally, the workflow action would be easier to reuse if all caller-specific matrix values were passed as explicit inputs, and the UNIX stream/seqpacket split would be clearer with a typed mode or separate helpers instead of a boolean selector.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -149,7 +151,7 @@
 
 Incorrect fallback (major): `matrix.release || true` turns the explicit `release: false` rows into `true`, because `false` is the left operand of `||`. The `syscall-debug` and `syscall-ext2-iommu-debug` jobs therefore run release builds instead of debug builds, and the same broken expression is repeated for the `gVisor` step at line `99`.
 
-**Fix.** Preserve explicit `false` values. For example, give every included matrix row an explicit `release` value and pass `release: ${{ matrix.release }}` in both integration-test steps.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other workflow fallback comment: preserve explicit `false` values by giving every included matrix row explicit defaults, then pass `release: ${{ matrix.release }}` and `enable_kvm: ${{ matrix.enable_kvm }}` in both integration-test steps.

 Reachable panic (major): A valid zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` receive reaches this `debug_assert!`: `send(..., len = 0)` queues an auxiliary range with `start == end`, `try_read()` pops it with `read_tot_len == 0`, and a non-empty receive buffer makes `is_empty` false. Debug builds then panic instead of returning `Ok((0, ...))`.

 ### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 315
 
@@ -203,7 +205,7 @@
 
 Lost readiness (major): `check_io_events()` reports `IN` only when the byte ring is non-empty, but a zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message is stored only in `peer_end.all_aux` with no bytes in `reader`. A task already blocked in `recvmsg()` or `poll()` can sleep forever after such a packet is sent, because the send path bypasses `write_with()` for `write_len == 0` and no `IN` readiness is observable.
 
-**Fix.** Represent zero-length sequenced packets in the readiness state and wake the peer when one is queued. For example, have `check_io_events()` treat a queued zero-length packet as `IoEvents::IN`, and notify the peer when `try_write()` pushes that packet.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` comment: represent zero-length sequenced packets in the receive/readiness state and notify the peer when one is queued. For example, have `check_io_events()` treat a queued zero-length packet as `IoEvents::IN`, and notify the peer when `try_write()` pushes that packet.

# Summary

The reviewed files keep most of the new behavior localized, but several issues are worth fixing before relying on this tree. The highest-impact problems are in the workflow defaults and the wait/socket edge cases: explicit `false` matrix values are converted back to `true`, the OSDK action branch reads the wrong matrix key, zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` messages are not represented in readiness, and `pause_timeout()` can miss an already-pending signal. The security-sensitive issue is that temporary syscall masks can include `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`, which Linux requires to remain unblockable.

Structurally, the workflow action would be easier to reuse if all caller-specific matrix values were passed as explicit inputs, and the UNIX stream/seqpacket split would be clearer with a typed mode or separate helpers instead of a boolean selector.

## Maintainability

> ```

`accurate-names` (nit): The workflow `name` literal is `Test x86-64  ` with two trailing spaces inside the string, so the Actions UI label differs from the visible name readers expect.

 ### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 113
 
@@ -191,7 +193,7 @@
 
 Reachable panic (major): A valid zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` receive reaches this `debug_assert!`: `send(..., len = 0)` queues an auxiliary range with `start == end`, `try_read()` pops it with `read_tot_len == 0`, and a non-empty receive buffer makes `is_empty` false. Debug builds then panic instead of returning `Ok((0, ...))`.
 
-**Fix.** Allow zero-length sequenced packets in the invariant, or remove this assertion. For example, include `is_seqpacket` in the condition: `debug_assert!(is_empty || is_seqpacket || read_tot_len != 0);`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` comment: represent zero-length sequenced packets in the receive/readiness state and notify the peer when one is queued. Then make this invariant allow the queued zero-length packet case, for example `debug_assert!(is_empty || is_seqpacket || read_tot_len != 0);`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 315
 
@@ -203,7 +205,7 @@
 
 Lost readiness (major): `check_io_events()` reports `IN` only when the byte ring is non-empty, but a zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message is stored only in `peer_end.all_aux` with no bytes in `reader`. A task already blocked in `recvmsg()` or `poll()` can sleep forever after such a packet is sent, because the send path bypasses `write_with()` for `write_len == 0` and no `IN` readiness is observable.
 
-**Fix.** Represent zero-length sequenced packets in the readiness state and wake the peer when one is queued. For example, have `check_io_events()` treat a queued zero-length packet as `IoEvents::IN`, and notify the peer when `try_write()` pushes that packet.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` comment: represent zero-length sequenced packets in the receive/readiness state and notify the peer when one is queued. For example, have `check_io_events()` treat a queued zero-length packet as `IoEvents::IN`, and notify the peer when `try_write()` pushes that packet.

===== EXPECTED DEFECTS =====
# Expected defects

1. location: .github/workflows/test_x86.yml lines 84-100 (persona: development, grounding: Preserve explicit false configuration values, severity: major)
   defect: The workflow passes `release` and `enable_kvm` to the test action with `${{ matrix.release || true }}` and `${{ matrix.enable_kvm || true }}`. In GitHub Actions expressions, an explicit boolean `false` is falsy, so `false || true` evaluates to `true`. Matrix entries that deliberately set `release: false` for debug builds or `enable_kvm: false` for non-KVM runs are therefore silently converted back to enabled/release runs, and CI does not execute the configuration declared by the matrix.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag that the `|| true` fallback overwrites explicit `false` values for `release` and `enable_kvm`, preventing debug and KVM-disabled CI jobs from actually running as requested.

2. location: kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs lines 202 (persona: development, grounding: Allow legal zero-length SOCK_SEQPACKET messages, severity: major)
   defect: `Connected::try_read` permits zero-length reads for `SOCK_SEQPACKET`, but the final invariant is `debug_assert!(is_empty || read_tot_len != 0)`. `is_empty` describes the caller's receive buffer, not whether the socket type allows zero-length packets. A valid empty `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message can therefore reach this assertion with `is_empty == false` and `read_tot_len == 0`, causing a deterministic debug-build panic instead of accepting the legal packet.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag that the assertion rejects legal zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` receives and ask for the invariant to be based on `is_seqpacket` or an equivalent protocol-aware condition.

3. location: kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs lines 152-175 (persona: development, grounding: Close the signal-registration to wait race, severity: major)
   defect: `Waiter::pause_timeout` installs the POSIX signal waker and then calls `self.wait()`, but it only checks `posix_thread.has_pending()` after the wait returns. If a signal is already pending before the waker is installed, no later signal wakeup is guaranteed, so `self.wait()` may sleep until the timeout or an unrelated wakeup. For calls such as `ppoll`, this can turn a pending `SIGINT` into a timeout instead of returning `EINTR`.
   MATCH IF: A reviewer should flag the lost-wakeup race between registering the signal waker and blocking, and require a pending-signal recheck plus waker cleanup on the early-return path.

# Summary

The reviewed files keep most of the new behavior localized, but several issues are worth fixing before relying on this tree. The highest-impact problems are in the workflow defaults and the wait/socket edge cases: explicit `false` matrix values are converted back to `true`, the OSDK action branch reads the wrong matrix key, zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` messages are not represented in readiness, and `pause_timeout()` can miss an already-pending signal. The security-sensitive issue is that temporary syscall masks can include `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`, which Linux requires to remain unblockable.

Structurally, the workflow action would be easier to reuse if all caller-specific matrix values were passed as explicit inputs, and the UNIX stream/seqpacket split would be clearer with a typed mode or separate helpers instead of a boolean selector.

## Maintainability

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 1

> ```diff
> 1	name: "Test x86-64  "
> ```

`accurate-names` (nit): The workflow `name` literal is `Test x86-64  ` with two trailing spaces inside the string, so the Actions UI label differs from the visible name readers expect.

**Fix.** Remove the trailing spaces, e.g. `name: Test x86-64`.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 18

> ```diff
> 17	      matrix:
> 18	        id: ['lint', 'compile', 'usermode_test', 'ktest']
> 22	      - name: Run basic tests
> 23	        uses: ./.github/actions/test
> 24	        with:
> 25	          auto_test: 'general'
> ```

`information-hiding` (minor): The `id` matrix axis is an implicit input to `.github/actions/test/action.yml`: the composite action reads `${{ matrix.id }}` directly, while this caller only passes `auto_test`. A future caller can rename `id` or reuse the action without discovering that hidden dependency from the `with` block.

**Fix.** Expose the selected basic test as an explicit action input, for example `basic_test: ${{ matrix.basic_test }}`, and have `.github/actions/test/action.yml` read `inputs.basic_test` instead of `matrix.id`.

### `.github/workflows/test_x86.yml` line 41

> ```diff
> 40	          # SMP Boot Test (Multiboot)
> 41	          - test_id: 'boot-multiboot2-smp4'
> 42	            boot_protocol: 'multiboot'
> 43	            smp: 4
> ```

`accurate-names` (minor): `test_id` says `boot-multiboot2-smp4`, but the adjacent comment and `boot_protocol` both say this case runs `multiboot`. That label will mislead anyone triaging boot protocol coverage from the matrix name.

**Fix.** Make the label and configuration agree: rename the case to `boot-multiboot-smp4` if `boot_protocol: 'multiboot'` is intended, or change `boot_protocol` and the comment to `multiboot2` if that is the intended test.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 102

> ```diff
> 99	    pub(super) fn try_read(
> 100	        {prompt}self,
> 101	        writer: {prompt}mut dyn MultiWrite,
> 102	        is_seqpacket: bool,
> 103	    ) -> Result<(usize, Vec<ControlMessage>)> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (major): `is_seqpacket` is a boolean behavior selector for `try_read` and `try_write`, and it drives separate stream-vs-seqpacket paths throughout the shared implementation. Call sites pass `self.is_seqpacket`, so the requested semantics are not clear without reading the callee.

**Fix.** Replace the flag with a typed mode such as `UnixStreamMode::{Stream, Seqpacket}`, or split the behavior into separate `try_read_stream`/`try_read_seqpacket` and `try_write_stream`/`try_write_seqpacket` helpers.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 238

> ```diff
> 234	        // Fast path: There are no auxiliary data to transmit.
> 235	        if aux_data.is_empty() {prompt}{prompt} !is_seqpacket {prompt}{prompt} !need_pass_cred {
> 236	            let mut writer = self.writer.lock();
> 237	            return self.inner.write_with(move || {
> 238	                if is_seqpacket {prompt}{prompt} writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens() {
> 239	                    return Ok(0);
> 240	                }
> 241	                writer.write_fallible(reader)
> 242	            });
> ```

`least-surprise` (minor): The fast path is entered only when `!is_seqpacket`, but the closure still checks `if is_seqpacket {prompt}{prompt} writer.free_len() < reader.sum_lens()`. That sub-branch is unreachable here and duplicates the real seqpacket capacity check in the slow path.

**Fix.** Remove the dead `is_seqpacket` check from this `!is_seqpacket` fast path, or factor the common write helper so the seqpacket capacity check appears only where seqpacket writes are possible.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 15

> ```diff
> 15	/// `Pause` is an extension trait to make [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`] signal aware.
> 16	///
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The first doc line for `Pause` is a sentence about the implementation form rather than a noun phrase for the trait. Rustdoc summaries for traits should read like a concise noun phrase.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summary as a noun phrase, for example `A signal-aware extension trait for [`Waiter`] and [`WaitQueue`].`

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 99

> ```diff
> 98	    #[track_caller]
> 99	    fn pause_timeout({prompt}self, timeout: {prompt}TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()>;
> 204	    fn pause_timeout({prompt}self, _timeout: {prompt}TimeoutExt<'_>) -> Result<()> {
> 205	        panic!("`pause_timeout` can only be used on `Waiter`");
> ```

`least-surprise` (major): `Pause` exposes `pause_timeout` for every implementor, but `WaitQueue::pause_timeout` below panics with `pause_timeout can only be used on Waiter`. A trait method that is intentionally unsupported forces callers to remember implementor-specific exceptions instead of trusting the trait contract.

**Fix.** Move `pause_timeout` to an inherent `Waiter` method or a waiter-only extension trait, or provide a real `WaitQueue` implementation instead of exposing an unsupported trait method.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 217

> ```diff
> 217	    // Save the original signal mask and apply the mask updates.
> 218	    let old_mask = sig_mask.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
> 219	    sig_mask.store(mask_op(old_mask), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 221	    // Perform the operation.
> 222	    let res = operate();
> 224	    // Restore the original signal mask.
> 225	    sig_mask.store(old_mask, Ordering::Relaxed);
> ```

`explain-why` (nit): The comments before `old_mask`, `operate()`, and `sig_mask.store(old_mask, ...)` restate exactly what the following statements do, without explaining a non-obvious rationale.

**Fix.** Remove these comments, or replace them with a single rationale comment only if there is a non-obvious signal-mask invariant that readers need to know.

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 244

> ```diff
> 244	        let boolean = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
> 245	        let boolean_cloned = boolean.clone();
> 255	        wait_queue
> 256	            .pause_until(|| boolean.load(Ordering::Relaxed).then_some(()))
> ```

`descriptive-names` (nit): `boolean` names the value's type instead of the condition being tested, so `boolean.load(Ordering::Relaxed).then_some(())` does not tell readers what state unblocks the waiter.

**Fix.** Rename `boolean` and `boolean_cloned` to the condition they represent, such as `is_woken` and `is_woken_cloned`.

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 202

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 315

Lost readiness (major): `check_io_events()` reports `IN` only when the byte ring is non-empty, but a zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` message is stored only in `peer_end.all_aux` with no bytes in `reader`. A task already blocked in `recvmsg()` or `poll()` can sleep forever after such a packet is sent, because the send path bypasses `write_with()` for `write_len == 0` and no `IN` readiness is observable.

**Fix.** Shared with the other zero-length `SOCK_SEQPACKET` comment: represent zero-length sequenced packets in the receive/readiness state and notify the peer when one is queued. ### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 154

> ```diff
> if let Some(posix_thread) = posix_thread_opt {
>     posix_thread.set_signalled_waker(self.waker());
>     self.wait();
>     posix_thread.clear_signalled_waker();
> } else {
>     self.wait();
> }
> ```

Missed pending signal (major): `pause_timeout()` installs the signal waker and immediately calls `self.wait()` without checking whether `posix_thread.has_pending()` is already true. If a signal became pending before `set_signalled_waker()`, no later signal enqueue occurs to wake this waiter; with `TimeoutExt::Never` from `Poller::new(None)`, `poll()`/`epoll_wait()` can block indefinitely instead of returning `EINTR`.

**Fix.** Check the cancel condition before sleeping, or reuse `wait_until_or_timeout_cancelled()` with a cancel closure that returns `EINTR` when `posix_thread.has_pending()` is true.

## Security

### `kernel/src/process/signal/pause.rs` line 219

> ```diff
>     let old_mask = sig_mask.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
>     sig_mask.store(mask_op(old_mask), Ordering::Relaxed);
> 
>     // Perform the operation.
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (major): `with_sigmask_changed` stores the temporary mask returned by `mask_op` verbatim. Callers such as `sys_ppoll` and `sys_pselect6` pass user-controlled masks, so a process can call `ppoll`/`pselect6` with `SIGKILL` or `SIGSTOP` set and then block indefinitely; local delivery uses `has_signal_blocked`/`has_pending`, so those mandatory signals remain pending instead of killing or stopping the thread. Linux documents that `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` cannot be blocked ([`sigprocmask(2)`](https://www.man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/sigprocmask.2.html), [`signal(7)`](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/signal.7.html)).

**Fix.** Strip `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` from the computed mask before storing it, matching `sys_rt_sigprocmask` and `sys_rt_sigsuspend`.

```rust
use super::constants::{SIGKILL, SIGSTOP};

let mut new_mask = mask_op(old_mask);
new_mask -= SIGKILL;
new_mask -= SIGSTOP;
sig_mask.store(new_mask, Ordering::Relaxed);
```

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/net/socket/unix/stream/connected.rs` line 276

> ```diff
> // Store the auxiliary data.
> let aux_range = RangedAuxiliaryData {
>     data: core::mem::take(aux_data),
>     start: write_start,
>     end: write_start + Wrapping(write_len),
> };
> all_aux.push_back(aux_range);
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (minor): `try_write()` stores `AF_UNIX` auxiliary data for delivery through `sendmsg(2)` and `recvmsg(2)`, including control-message behavior such as `SCM_RIGHTS`, `SCM_CREDENTIALS`, and automatic credentials from `SO_PASSCRED`. That enhances a user-visible syscall surface, but the review input has no matching Linux compatibility coverage update.

**Fix.** Update the Linux Compatibility syscall coverage for `sendmsg(2)` and `recvmsg(2)` to document the newly supported `AF_UNIX` ancillary-message behavior, and add or update the matching `.scml` coverage cases in the same change.
